The マップモード-only 艦艇配置 panel (`creative/ship-placer-panel.ts`) places ships by orbital elements or by a Lagrange-point halo/Lissajous orbit (see `src/physics/halo.ts` above for why the latter drifts once the simulation runs); a placed ship's right-click menu (`kind: 'player'` in `MapContextActions.itemsFor`) opens with 軌道計画の実行 — a single item cycling OFF→瞬間移動→自動操縦→OFF on each click (`keepOpen: true`, so the window stays open across clicks; label shows the current mode, `plan/plan-executor.ts`'s `PlanExecutionMode` above), shown only while `activeStage.executesPlans` — a stage with no driver for `PlanExecutor`/the `'instant'` teleport would offer a control with no effect) — followed by 操作対象にする/操作対象を解除 (`activePlayers.set`/`setOrNull(null)`, one label replacing the other by whether this ship is already active — deactivating leaves `Game.player` `null`, the same "no ship flown" state a total wipe-out on this stage leaves), フォーカスを移動, 航法ターゲットに設定/解除 (`NavTarget`, same `navTargetItems` helper the `'body'`/`'ship'` kinds use), and 削除 — the last is simply absent from the menu for the currently-active ship (deleting it would leave `player` dangling).

The orbital direction markers (`player/player-markers.ts`) deliberately carry no key hints in their labels: combat-view translation is body-frame, so none of those six directions has a key. The keys that *do* mean prograde/normal/radial are the map-mode Δv edit keys, and the direction markers are hidden there.

Chase camera reference frame (`camFollowAttitude` on `ChaseCamera`, default **on**, toggled via `[G]`, which `CombatCameraSystem.update` reads directly and forwards as `chaseCamera.toggleFollowAttitude(player)` — so it's only live while combat view is active): the third-person camera's orientation is stored as a single quaternion offset (`rot`) from a reference frame, but *which* frame is configurable. ON (default) references the ship's own attitude (`player.att.q`), so rotating the ship with I/K/J/L/U/O visibly swings the whole camera (including its roll) right along with it, RCS-linked. OFF references ECI directly, so the camera holds a fixed orientation in inertial space regardless of how the ship rotates, until dragged. Toggling reinterprets `rot` through the ship's current attitude so the view doesn't jump. While the ship is destroyed, ON stops folding in the ship's attitude (which would otherwise be its post-destruction tumble) and holds the last orientation instead. `CombatCameraSystem` itself only decides between `ChaseCamera` and `GunsightCamera` (via `zoomActive`) and animates the FOV each holds in its own `view`.

Ammo system: guns fire alternately from the two front bores and consume magazine rounds (`MAG_ROUNDS`=32/mag, `INITIAL_MAGS`=3 linked at start, `MAGS_PER_BARREL`=3 consumed before a barrel-swap reload — doubling the per-mag capacity from an original 16-round design let the visible chain be roughly half as long while carrying comparable total ammo). The belt of unused mags hangs off the ship's port (+X) side as child meshes of `player.obj`, and the belt slides continuously into the hull as rounds are fired (`beltFeed` = 1 − rounds/`MAG_ROUNDS` offsets the anchor by up to one pitch; on mag consumption the feed wraps 1→0 exactly when the link count drops, so the motion reads as seamless). The head link sits half-inside the hull. `BeltPhysics.update` (in `player/belt-physics.ts`) simulates the belt's sag as an actual physics chain, not a canned animation: link joints are point masses (`beltPos`/`beltPrevPos`, Verlet-integrated in the ship's body frame) connected by rigid-rod distance constraints (position-based dynamics, 6 relaxation iterations per frame) to `MAG_BELT_PITCH`. Since the ship is in free fall, ordinary gravity can't sag anything (equivalence principle — belt and hull fall together); the only real driver is the fictitious force felt in the ship's non-inertial (accelerating/rotating) frame: `-a_thrust - α×r - ω×(ω×r) - 2ω×v` (translational inertia, Euler, centrifugal, Coriolis), built from `thrustAccelVec` (world-frame thrust accel, rotated into body frame, passed in each call from `Player.behave` via `Belt.update`) and `player.att.w` (with `α` estimated by finite-differencing `w` against `prevShipW`, both tracked inside `BeltPhysics`). When the player isn't thrusting or rotating, the chain just hangs straight — no ambient wobble is faked in.

Each link's *orientation* is derived separately from its position, via parallel transport: `qBend = qDelta * qPrev` where `qDelta` is the minimal (zero-twist) rotation taking the previous link's local +X to this link's tangent — so bending (the chain folding link-to-link) is unconstrained by construction, matching a real ammo belt's easy up/down/left/right flex. Roll (twist about the link's own +X, the one DOF parallel transport leaves undefined) is instead tracked explicitly per link in `beltTwist[]`, driven by the ship's own roll rate (`player.att.w.z * MAG_CHAIN_ROLL_GAIN`) at link 0 and propagated down the chain with a one-frame lag (`prevTwist`) and exponential smoothing (`MAG_CHAIN_ROLL_RATE`), clamped every frame to `±MAG_CHAIN_MAX_ROLL_DEG` — so however it's driven, twist can never exceed that bound. The final per-link quaternion is `qBend * qTwist(local +X)`.

`ContactPhysics` (in `game/simulation/contact.ts`; rigid-sphere contact for casings/enemies/pickups/radiator folds/belt) is driven from `Simulator.advance` in two cadences: `resolveSubstep` runs once per substep alongside the integration, while `resolveBelt` runs once per frame at the real (unwarped) `dt`, since the belt is a ship-local simulation on its own real-time cadence. Both are gated on `simSpeedManager.canResolvePhysicalCollisions` — at higher warp contact is skipped entirely, since substeps up to 20s each make rigid-body contact resolution meaningless. The ship's contact and hit radius are the one `PLAYER_HULL_RADIUS` (real hull size, 2.6m), so a freshly-ejected casing (spawned ~2.9m from the hull) clears it rather than being flung outward on the same frame. A casing/hull impact plays `WorldSfx.clank()` from the colliding entity's own `collideWith` (`DebrisPiece` for the casing side, `Player` for the hull side), not from the contact resolver.

When mags run low (`LOGISTICS_LOW_MAGS`), resupply pickups spawn phase-shifted 1.25–2.5 km ahead on the player's own orbit (`stage-utils/logistics.ts`'s `Logistics.spawnForPlayer`; ▣ 弾薬 marker, absorb within `AMMO_PICKUP_RADIUS`). Casings use a CIWS-style bottleneck profile (rim/extractor groove/tapered body/shoulder/neck via `LatheGeometry`) with **low** ejection speed but widely varying per-casing spin, on an asymmetric inertia tensor so the Dzhanibekov effect shows on tumbling casings the same way it does on debris. Spin-up SFX is filtered-noise friction (no oscillator). Enemy ships are modeled at `ENEMY_SCALE` (20×) via `obj.scale.setScalar(...)` with `ENEMY_RADIUS` (180 m) matching for hit detection; their destruction VFX (`spawnFlash` sizes, `scatterFragments` size range) scale by the same factor so the explosion reads proportionate to the larger hull.

Mouse buttons are swapped from the naive default: **left button drags the camera; a left click that moves less than `CLICK_MOVE_THRESHOLD` (6px) is instead queued as a click** (in map mode: selects the self-flown ship/base it lands on — see `MapContextActions.handleLeftClick` — or, failing that, places/selects a maneuver node) — see `Input.clicks()`. **Right button also drags the camera** (feeding the same `dx`/`dy` as the left drag — a two-finger trackpad drag, since right-click has no natural trackpad equivalent), and a right click within `CLICK_MOVE_THRESHOLD` (`Input.pushIfClick`, shared across left/middle/right) instead opens a target/context menu. Firing is Space-only now — right click no longer fires. This lets map-mode node placement and camera dragging share the left button, and camera dragging and menu-opening share the right button, without a modifier key.

Map mode ([M]) does not freeze physics: `Simulator.advance` runs every frame while `activeStage.isPlaying && !paused`, and neither firing nor manual rotation (RCS, IJKLUO) is gated by map mode — neither shares a key with Δv editing. Translation (WASDQE) goes to whichever owner claims it first: with no node selected, WASDQE fire the real engines exactly as in combat view; with one selected, `PlanEditor.updateEditing` claims those six keys through `Input.takeHeld` in the input phase, so they edit that node's Δv (prograde/normal/radial, not body axes) and `Player` sees them as un-pressed for the frame. So the player can fly for real while the map is open, and only loses direct thrust control while actively shaping a burn. Only the pointer dispatch is layered on top of `update`; the map-mode panels (表示 / PREDICT / 座標系 / 軌道オブジェクト) show and refresh themselves from their owners' `sync`. The map camera's drag yaw sign is intentionally opposite the combat chase camera's convention (`+=` vs `-=` on `mouse.dx`) so panning left/right reads the same in both views.

Each of the six translation keys can be **latched** by pressing it twice within `THRUST_LATCH_DOUBLE_TAP_SEC` (`PlayerThrottle`'s `updateThrustLatches`/`isThrustHeld`): a latched key keeps firing that thruster as if held, independent of the physical key state, until double-tapped again to release it. This lets sustained burns run without holding a key down — useful in particular while the map is open and the mouse is busy dragging the camera/plan. Latches are per-key (not per-axis), cleared on `clearTransientState` (deactivating/parking the ship); holding a key's opposite direction also holds that key's latch cleared for as long as the opposite key stays down, so latching W and then holding S gives reverse thrust instead of the two canceling out, and releasing S leaves W's latch gone rather than reviving it. Above `MAX_PHYS_SIM_SPEED` (where thrust doesn't apply anyway) double-tapping neither latches nor consumes the press, so a latch can't accumulate silently during high warp and surprise-fire once warp drops back down. Holding **any** opposite pair together — W+S, A+D, or Q+E — is an emergency all-stop: it zeroes thrust on *every* axis (not just the held pair's own) and clears every latch, regardless of what else is held or latched at the time.
